# Roadmap

The live board of every development item the plan, its ADRs and its experiments name: what is done and what closed it, what is under way, what is open, what is blocked and by what, and what a recorded decision has deferred. Each row is a title and a pointer; what the item is, and why, lives at the pointer. The history of estimates against actuals stays in the ledger ([10 §10.8](plan/10-roadmap-risks.md#108-calibrating-the-estimates)); this page is the current state.

**Keeping it current.** A change that finishes, starts or blocks a roadmap item updates its row here in the same commit, and a new piece of work gets a row before it starts ([AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#rules-that-are-checked), "Documentation moves with the code"). `tools/docs-check.ps1` holds the page to its shape: ids are unique, every id a "Depends on" cell names is a row, a `done` row names what closed it, a `blocked` row says by what, every row has a Where link that resolves, and State, Effort and Reasoning use the words below. Those rules read item tables only (a table whose header row starts with `#`), and a table ends at its first line that is not a row, so **an item table has no blank line inside it**: the rows after one would be a table with no header that nothing reads, and the check refuses any row whose first cell is an item id outside an item table, by its line (T115; forty rows went unchecked that way until 2026-11-11).

## How to read it

| Column | Values |
|---|---|
| `#` | A stable id: the area's letter and a number. An id is never renumbered or reused, so a dependency can name it; a new item takes its area's next free number wherever its row goes. |
| Item | The title. |
| State | `done` (with the ADR, commit or dated status note that closed it), `in progress`, `open`, `blocked` (by what), `deferred` (a decision is recorded not to build it now). |
| Depends on | The ids it needs first. Empty means it can start now. |
| Effort | `S`: hours, one agent's stage. `M`: a day, one agent. `L`: several agents or batches. |
| Reasoning | `routine`: the pattern exists in the tree and the spec is clear, a smaller model's task. `design`: choices to make, trade-offs to weigh. `research`: unknowns, and an experiment decides. |
| Where | The plan section, ADR, experiment or page to read first. |

**Blocked and open.** A row is `blocked` only by something outside ordinary work: the owner's review or decision, hardware, a runner, a session on the owner's machine. A row that waits on other rows is `open`, and its "Depends on" says on what. The work that can start today is therefore every `open` row whose dependencies are all `done`.

**Done is a claim with a source.** A `done` row names the ADR, the commit or the dated status note that closed it. Where only part of a plan item is built, the built part is a `done` row and the named remainder an `open` one. A proposed ADR whose decision is built shows twice: the built item, `done`, and its acceptance, `blocked` on the owner's review.

**Areas**, in roughly the order the plan builds things: [R, the renderer core and ray-traced lighting](#r--the-renderer-core-and-ray-traced-lighting); [W, the world, its streaming and the ground](#w--the-world-its-streaming-and-the-ground); [D, the data model and documents](#d--the-data-model-and-documents); [S, simulation and physics](#s--simulation-and-physics); [C, characters and tissue](#c--characters-and-tissue); [G, content and generation](#g--content-and-generation); [T, tooling and CI](#t--tooling-and-ci); [A, the agent protocol and tools](#a--the-agent-protocol-and-tools); [P, the reference games](#p--the-reference-games); [F, found along the way](#f--found-along-the-way), the open defects.

Compiled on 2026-10-07 from the plan's thirteen chapters and their dated status notes and addenda, the §10.8 ledger, the [ADR index](adr/README.md), the [experiment index](experiments/README.md), the subsystem pages' "Not yet" lists, and the git log since 2026-09-05.

Batch 67's 2.1 GB on the village walk is the chain's **first capacity, a guess clamped to the budget, held**: 330,125 clusters for frames that build at most 6,457, and the budget is a sixteenth of the card, so the number said what the card is. | R69 | Radiance cache and ReSTIR GI, staged (the owner asked for the environment's light, 2026-11-02): stage 1 is the world-space cache alone — a hash grid or surfels over the frame's visible surfaces, fed by a budgeted number of rays a frame against the frame's structures (the traversal the sun's ray already pays, so the cost is the ray budget: the primary trace is 15.4 ms for 24.9 million rays at full width on the forest, R127, so a cache fed by one or two million incoherent rays a frame is a few milliseconds), one bounce lit by the sun, the sky and the cache itself, read per pixel in place of the nine-harmonic ambient, converging in world space rather than in a screen-space history, the reference path tracer the judge of what it recovers; ReSTIR GI is stage 2 and needs R68, stage 1 does not; the water's mirror is R129's ray, not the cache's. **Stage 1 in progress (batch 54, 2026-11-07)**: the cache (29f26212) and the bounce (c6301ed8) behind `renderer.gi.mode`, off by default — `bounce` beats the harmonics against the reference but costs 6.9 ms of the 3840×2160 walk, 2.8 of it the read; next the read's cost and the blocks a rounded rock shows ([renderer](subsystems/renderer.md#indirect-light-the-world-space-cache), [the judging](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md)). **Stage 2 of the cache (batch 55, 2026-11-08), in progress**: the keys apart in 32-byte bucket blocks, the young cell blended with its parent, a full bucket's stale cells freed, half the rays to young cells, a hit's coarser levels and a bounded fallback, the faces' blend near a face's edge, each a knob judged against stage 1 ([stage 2](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md#stage-2-the-fill-the-blocks-the-brightness-the-read)); the default stays off. **Stage 2b (batch 56, 2026-11-08), in progress**: the quiet cost table taken (the read is 2.8 ms of the v35 resolve at 3840×2160 and the sample pass 2.2, against targets of 1.5 and 2; [stage 2b](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md#stage-2b-the-quiet-costs-2026-11-08-batch-56-in-progress)); the forest's luminance against the reference, the budgets with `bounce` on and the surround runs wait on the GPU lock; the read at half rate in the side columns is a structure change, requested. **Stage 3 (batch 57) held at the gate, 2026-11-09**: `b57-R69-3` (5decb5ab, on eeb8077c) built the turn's measurement from the v36 cave mouth, a read cascade over five levels, probes round the eye, the read's integers without a 64-bit division, a wave-shared read and two tail knobs, all off by default because none met the bound; the gate's first GPU run of its tests (its own had queued on the GPU lock) found the **defaults** 8 colour steps apart under a move by whole cells where eeb8077c draws 0 and the cache off draws 0, the cascade 57 apart and the probes 8, and the turn test asserting a fill the cascade does not reach (under a twentieth of its first distance within 64 frames; stage 2's read does from frame 55). Batch 58's first R69 item, from that branch rebased: the defaults byte-identical to stage 2b and invariant first (the read's integers and the shared corner hashes are the suspects), then the cascade's invariance (its coarse levels' cells against the 64 m world cell), then the tail. **Stage 4 (batch 58, 2026-11-09), in progress**: batch 58 took the owner's v37 turn instead of stage 3's branch (left unmerged, its section carried to [the page](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md#stage-3-the-fill-on-a-turn-and-the-reads-cost) as a negative result): a caster volume round the eye (`renderer.gi.shell_m`, `eye_shell.h`) and samples over the sphere from the eye (`renderer.gi.shell_rays`), both off by default — on the v37 `south-lake` turn they took four fifths of the bias away but not the fill (1.68 → 1.76 against a no-turn floor of 0.43: the variance of few-sample cells), and cost 0.8 ms at 3840×2160 at 48 m; in the box from inside the first frame after a turn is 0.018 of the term from converged against main's 0.071 ([stage 4](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md#stage-4-the-light-round-the-eye)); the cache's counts now fold on pipelined frames, and they showed the 32 MiB cache full on v37, so `renderer.gi.cache_mib` defaults to 128, which alone takes a fifth of the turn's fill away (1.68 → 1.30). Next: about ten times the samples' rays to warm the sphere's own level, the samples' rays off the young half while the eye moves, the turn's metric against a long-held reference, and the bake for the start. **Stage 5 (batch 59, 2026-11-10), in progress**: the owner's third report of the same sentence (v40: `the problem is the filling being visible at all`), so the target is no change the world did not cause, measured in time against a long-held reference ([stage 5](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md#stage-5-the-light-is-already-there), [the design](subsystems/renderer.md#stage-5-the-light-is-already-there)): every sample to four consecutive levels and a read that walks them, each level taken by the samples a cell of its mean holds over 255 (`renderer.gi.confident`, 32 before), so no level adds more noise than a converged cell's times 1.42 and a filling cell changes frame to frame as a filled one; a cell's history 256 samples or sixteen frames of them (a coarse cell kept none of its past); stage 4's volume and samples round the eye on by default, added to the three levels above a surface's own; a pre-roll of 32 sample-and-fold pairs on the cache's first frame and on a frame whose eye jumped 8 m (`gi_preroll.h`); and a terrain module of the cache's passes, so a bounce off the ground takes the ground's own layers (R129's hand-off). Found on the way: main's light moves again 240 frames after a turn, when the cells behind the eye age out (the samples round the eye hold them). **Where it stands (b1493aec, 1dfc1862 and the measurements' commit)**: in the box a turn, a cut and a load from its twenty-fourth frame stand inside twice the cache's steady floor where the plain read stands eighteen floors off; **on the v40 forest the target is not met** — the turn's speckle is gone from the ground and the near trees and its change between frames is a third less (0.63 against 0.92, the floor's 0.18), the flight's dark start is gone, but the far forest's first frames are one band brighter than it will be (+0.58 of a luma step over the picture, twenty frames), which is the coarse levels' own light and not only the wait for the fine ones (it is +0.39 at a count of 64), and the stage costs 1.4 to 2.4 ms a frame at 3840×2160 `rt` (0.6 to 1.6 of read: the crowns' cells never hold 255 samples and walk two to four levels). Not measured: the forest's load, `forest-ground` and `cave-mouth` under stage 5, a 1920×1080 pair, and anything with the clouds held still (the curves carry their drift; the `off` run died of the machine's memory). Next: the coarse levels' bias against their children, the read's cost on crowns (a cap of three levels, the stop at an eighth), a fold that forgets the pre-roll's first rounds, and the cache's size at 3840×2160 (3.0 of 4.2 million cells held, 300 inserts a frame turned away). **Stage 6 (2026-11-11, batch 60: stage 5 merged onto be3826d9, then the stage's commit)**: the band counted and its cause found — the coarse levels hold the same light as the fine ones (within 0.05 % in linear light, read one level at a time with the new diagnostic `renderer.gi.show_level`), flattened (a crown's shade 1.6 times as bright three levels up), and the display's curve makes a flattened picture a brighter one, so nothing that corrects a coarse cell's mean touches it (three such corrections measured doing nothing); **the reveal** (`renderer.gi.reveal`, on): a level not yet read whole by its count takes what its difference from the next level up shows beyond the two means' noise (a shrinkage weight with the prior's spread estimated by its positive part), which is nothing where the levels agree and never more noise than the difference shown; on the grove's turn the band is +0.18 of a step over the crowns against stage 5's +0.29 and main's +0.06 (the floor 0.27), the distance 0.88 against 0.99 and main's 0.67, the frames' change 0.097 against 0.091; cost at 3840×2160 `rt` on the grove, alternating pairs: 0.15 ms over main on crowns, 0.18 under it on sand, 0.03 over stage 5 (whose forest figure of 1.4 to 2.4 the grove does not show). On the v41 forest at 3840×2160 `rt` with the owner's wind (three runs: main's and stage 6's turn as sequences, stage 6 held at `south-lake` under `--benchmark`): the band is inside what the wind does to the picture for both (+0.19 and +0.23 of a step, the floor 11.5) and no still of the turn shows a fill in either; stage 6 costs about 1.4 ms a frame over main there (17.5 against 16.1, not an alternated pair: `gi_trace` +0.5, the read +0.46, the chain +0.23), as stage 5 did; and stages 5 and 6 draw the forest a tenth darker than main on every frame (8 of 80 8-bit steps over the trees), most likely stage 5's bounce off the ground's own layers, not isolated and not judged against the reference. **Not met: the band is a third smaller, not gone, and main stands nearer on far crowns; the cost on the forest is 1.4 ms, not half a millisecond; nobody has watched the two at speed.** **Stage 7** (2026-11-12, batch 61, on main 33ff2824; [renderer](subsystems/renderer.md#stage-7-a-crowns-shade-as-a-prior), [the measurements](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md#stage-7-a-crowns-shade-as-a-prior), [the grounding](experiments/crown-shade-grounding.md)): **the darker forest judged** — the reference tracer on the grove over a layered, textured ground has main at 1.47 of it over the trees and stages 5 and 6 at 1.05 (the ground 1.07 for both), so stage 5's darker picture is the right one: main's bounce off a layered ground reads none of its layers. **A crown's shade as a prior**: each mesh with a crown carries a grid of the mean fraction of diffuse light its own leaves let through (Beer-Lambert in leaf area, G = 0.5, from the mesh's finest alpha-tested cards at load; no byte a vertex; a distance form reads its source's); foliage is keyed by no face, its cells hold the samples' light over the sum of their priors, and a foliage pixel reads one coarse level times its own prior. On the grove at 3840×2160 traced the frame over crowns is 0.2 ms **under** main's (the resolve 1.90 against 2.11 and stage 6's 2.28); on the turn the first frame stands 0.39 of a step from the long-held picture (main 0.72, stage 6 0.99, the floor 0.29) and the band is +0.07, inside the floor. On the forest (v41, `south-lake` held, two flights) it costs **1.6 ms a frame over main** (20.3 against 18.8 ms: the sample pass +0.5, the resolve +0.6, the chain and the primaries +0.5), where the target was half a millisecond: 93 of its 14,749 meshes have a crown, and the far forest, the sward, the samples round the eye and their volume are as stages 5 and 6 left them; its forest is 9 steps darker than main's over vegetation, the side the judge says is right; the forest turn's band was not measured. **Not met or not done**: the forest's cost; a prior that is wrong shows on a turn's first frame (the stand of cubes: 3.7 steps darker for seven frames); against the reference a crown's shaded leaves are still twice too bright with every read (1.9 to 2.1 with the prior, 1.7 to 1.9 in stage 6, 2.8 to 3.0 in main); the still picture's change between frames over far crowns is 0.14 of a step against main's 0.08; the young half's hand-out is still the order threads reach a count; one G and one card fill for every species. Next: why shaded leaves stand at twice the reference (the reference at one bounce says it is the sky's visibility, not the bounce); a longer memory for a crown's cells; the prior's march in the tree's content build with the solver's own leaf area and angle; the young half by pixel and frame; then the cache's size at 3840×2160, the rock test's margin and the read's step at a power of two, all untouched. **Stage 8** (2026-11-12, batch 62; [renderer](subsystems/renderer.md#stage-8-what-the-forest-paid-for-and-what-a-far-crown-remembers), [the measurements](experiments/gi-cache-2026-11-07.md#stage-8-the-forests-cost-by-term-the-far-crowns-flicker)): **the forest's cost split before any cut** — stage 7's binary flown with one thing switched a flight, main beside it: the gap is 0.75 to 1.0 ms in one machine state (the "1.6" was two flights of a slower state), the sample pass +0.48, the resolve +0.37, the chain +0.19, the primaries +0.17. On the grove at 3840×2160 traced the frame is 0.3 ms under main's over crowns and 0.2 under it over sand, and the picture stands where stage 7's did against the reference (1.07 over the trees). A two-sided card has one base colour for both faces, and the resolve turns its shading normal to the eye's side, so a leaf the wind has flipped shows its top's colour lit as a top. A real broadleaf's lower face is commonly paler and duller than its upper (hairs, wax, less chlorophyll near that face), which is why a wood turns silvery before a storm. A property any two-sided material may declare, as the foliage optics are (a back face's base colour, or its reflectance as a ratio of the front's; the material's own record and a glTF extra, keyed on the asset and never on a generator), read by every reader of the foliage functions: the resolve, the traced hit's shading, the bounce's cache, the reference path tracer, and `brdf_reference.h` in the same commit. Zero is the stand-in: a material that declares none draws what it drew. To read before a number is pinned: published upper against lower reflectance spectra for the forest's broadleaf species, on the grounding page. To settle on the way: which face of a card is the upper one (the winding's front, so the flora solver's cards and a bought plant's must agree, and a case holds them to it), and what the transmitted share takes its colour from. **The grounding stands** (2026-11-25, [leaf faces and reconfiguration](experiments/leaf-faces-and-reconfiguration-grounding.md)): every read source has a broadleaf's lower face brighter in the visible, and Hovi et al. found its visible transmittance not affected by the side (the one read source that measured it); the felted leaves (white poplar, silver maple, whitebeam) the most two-coloured; no ratio by band was found as text, so the row starts by reducing the CC BY 4.0 leaf library that measured both faces; no number is pinned | open | R164 | M | design | [renderer](subsystems/renderer.md#foliage-lit-from-both-sides) |

A mesh with no authored coordinates (a city's surveyed buildings, whose faces turn every way) has to be given them a vertex at a time, with a seam at every corner and a stretch on every slope; and coordinates reach the card as half floats, about 0.15 m at the top of a 300 m tower when they are in metres. A projection a material may ask for (by the surface's own normal, blended at the turns), and the coordinate's storage looked at again for large meshes | open | | M | design | [renderer](subsystems/renderer.md) |
| R237 | A shader that must not contract says so in its SPIR-V (W175's finding, 2026-11-26). As the tree compiles shaders, Slang's `precise` leaves no `NoContraction` decoration: W175 found none in the module it had marked, and slangc's `-fp-mode precise` put them there. Two shaders rest on exact arithmetic and say so with `precise`: the key `clas_records.slang` sorts by (ADR-0074), and `instance_from_eye` in `scene.slang`, which `world_eye_tests.cpp` holds to `engine::relative` to the bit; if the finding stands, both hold on today's drivers by the driver's choice and not by the module. [gfx](subsystems/gfx.md) says the three statements are `precise`, SPIR-V's NoContraction, and [renderer](subsystems/renderer.md) says otherwise. First confirm it by reading the two modules' SPIR-V; then settle what the compile passes (the mode for the modules that claim exactness, or the tree's, its cost measured on the budgets' flights), hold it by a test that reads the SPIR-V for the decoration wherever a shader claims it, and make the two pages say one thing | open | | S | design | [gfx](subsystems/gfx.md) |
| R238 | The BSDF returns no more light than it receives (found by R234's independent read, 2026-11-26). The whole albedo of the model in `brdf.slang`, its diffuse lobe and its specular together, passes 1 toward grazing: 1.71 at roughness 0.1 with a white base, as that read computed it, on main and before R234 changes anything. The likely cause is the common one, a diffuse lobe that does not give up what the specular reflects, and the remedies are published (the diffuse weighted by what Fresnel leaves; a compensation for the microfacets' multiple scattering). Grounding first: the sources read, the albedo tabulated by angle and roughness for the present model and for each remedy, its cost a pixel stated. Then the model and `brdf_reference.h` in the same commit, a white-furnace case that holds the albedo at or under 1, and every pinned picture that moves attributed in a sentence | open | | M | research | [gfx](subsystems/gfx.md) |

A capability of its own (ADR-0027), with no renderer and no card: a well-balanced, positivity-preserving finite-volume step for depth and momentum over a height field that may itself change, with sources, bed friction, the ground's store and a carried surface quantity for the foam, the same bytes twice. Held by cases with known answers: a lake at rest stays at rest over any bed; a dam's break against the closed forms, on a wet bed and a dry one; a bowl's sloshing shoreline; water counted to the last cell through wetting, drying and the ground's take; a source filling a hollow to its lip and spilling at the level the steady solve gives (W176's check). **2026-11-25**: the capability `water_state` stands ([water_state](subsystems/water_state.md)): a first-order hydrostatic-reconstruction step with the central-upwind flux in `double`, wetting and drying, the bed and its roughness inputs of every step, Manning friction, sources, the ground's store by Green and Ampt's rate, carried quantities on the one transport path with a source, a decay, the foam's source from the step's own dissipation (a bore's included) and a hook for the exchange with the bed, open and closed edges and a ledger. Green: the lake at rest (exact over a bed of binary fractions), Stoker's and Ritter's dam breaks, the count of water through wetting, drying and the ground, Green and Ampt's closed form, the changed bed, two carried quantities, a bounded concentration, the bore's dissipation against Bélanger's, Thacker's bowl, the same bytes at 1 and 4 threads and a pinned hash. The hollow (W176's geometry): critical flow on the lip holds, settled, counted, from both starts; **the lake stands 38 % (Q 0.1) and 48 % (Q 0.02) of E_c too high**, a head the hydrostatic reconstruction loses at the hollow's upstream bed step under moving water: it hands the face the lake's speed on the shallower depth, and the mass flux's numerical viscosity makes up the discharge with a depth difference of about q Δz / (h_L ∣a⁻∣), with no Δx in it, so refining does not help (the 1-D case pins it within 25 %; W176's 5 % bound is held as an expected failure). The reference is not to be trusted for steady flow over a bed that steps by a fraction of the depth or more. Not done: a reconstruction that keeps moving steady states at a bed discontinuity (Berthon and Michel-Dansac's, to be read first), W176's graded hollow, a steady flow over a smooth bump against its closed form, second order, an edge that makes waves, the store's drainage, the exchange's law (W178) | in progress | W173 | M | design | [water_state](subsystems/water_state.md), [water state](experiments/water-state-grounding.md) |

A sky takes a latitude, a day of the year and a moon's age typed by hand, and its hour is local solar time, so a photograph's place and clock time are converted outside the engine before a capture can be set beside it. A longitude, a date with its year and a time with its offset from UTC, from which the provider's ephemeris gives the sun's and the moon's places and the moon's age; a scene that names none of them draws what it drew | done (2026-11-25, batch 76: `Sky` version 4's `longitude_deg`, `date`, `time_of_day_h` and `utc_offset_h`, all four or none; the provider "earth" runs the same sun and moon at the moment's own days from J2000.0 and turns the sky by Greenwich mean sidereal time plus the longitude, the drawing unchanged; the scene's moment starts the sky's clock, the date runs on through midnight, `--time-of-day` is that clock's hour; a hand-typed day, moon age or `terrain.time` beside it refused naming what to drop; against JPL Horizons' airless tables at Toronto, Sydney and Tromsø the sun within 0.01° and the moon's lit fraction within 0.01 ([grounding](experiments/sky-by-place-and-civil-time.md)); refraction and the moon's parallax still not drawn, a game's calendar W187's) | | M | design | [sky](subsystems/sky.md#the-sky-by-place-and-civil-time) |
| W186 | A scene says where on the Earth it is (the Toronto proof of concept's gap G-T6): the projected coordinate system, the origin and the height datum its world coordinates are in, as fields of the scene that tools read and the engine carries without interpreting, so tiles built by different runs agree and a world position turns back into a map coordinate | done (2026-11-25, batch 76: `engine.scene.Georeference`, the scene's optional `georeference` (`Scene` version 14): the horizontal and vertical systems by name (an authority and code, or WKT), the world origin's easting, northing and height in f64, the engine axes east and north lie along, the map unit's length; its form checked by the scene reader, which refuses a malformed one with the file's path, and carried unread to `SceneData` and `render.scenes` (`RenderLoadedScene` version 3); `scene_gen::map_from_world` and `world_from_map`, the round trip from the map back to its bits at a national grid's magnitudes; a scene with no block reads and draws as it did. Not done: no tile-building tool compares two runs' blocks yet, and no case covers the field `render.scenes` prints, which needs a device) | | S | design | [scene_gen](subsystems/scene_gen.md#where-on-the-earth-a-scene-is) · [grounding](experiments/georeference-grounding.md) · [gfx](subsystems/gfx.md#the-frames-origin) |
| W187 | A game's own time scales: the day's length, and the year's apart from it (the owner, 2026-11-25: a long game is 100 to 200 hours of play and 40 or 50 in-game days, "if even", yet the span it covers is "a year or two"; "each game pretty much needs to have it's own timescales that feel right, both the day/night cycle timescale, but then also the months-years timescale too which is decoupled from the day/night unlike reality"). ADR-0056 gave the world two clocks, the world's rate for everything that moves and the day's length for the sky's hour. This is a third, the calendar: how far the year advances in a day of play, set by the game and independent of the day's length, so forty days can walk through two years of seasons. Decide who reads which: the sun's path through the year and the moon's age (beside W185's civil date), the seasons' foliage and snow, a stream's seasonal inflow, and the slow change of a river's bed and a shore's section (W178, W180) read the calendar; what moves in front of the eye (waves, a flood's front, a falling tree) stays on the world clock; and an event may carry a strength of its own, the owner's case being a hurricane that reshapes a shore, within bounds, inside its own few hours. The calendar moves two ways: by its rate, and by a transition the game declares (the owner, the same evening: "x amount of time has gone by and it's winter now for example, regardless of how long the player spent up to that point"), a jump to a date however much was played. So whatever reads the calendar can be asked for its state at a date without living through the gap: a baked family or a closed form by date for the slow things, and a running state started again from the steady answer for the new season. A replay and a save carry all three | open | | M | design | [renderer](subsystems/renderer.md#one-world-clock-and-the-days-length) |

| S35 | Animation graphs as data | in progress (2026-11-18, b69-S35: designed, nothing built, [the grounding and the design](experiments/animation-graphs.md): a graph is document records (states, transitions, nodes of a closed set of kinds) compiled per graph and skeleton into a control table and a pose program evaluated op by op over batches of its instances; inputs and state are two persisted components of 56 and 64 bytes; inertialization after Bollo 2017; a far resident's looping pose shared at LOD1 by phase bucket; hot reload through the change feed. 2026-11-18, b69-S35-1: **stage 1 built** — `animation_graph.schema`'s six record types and two components (56 and 64 bytes, size-tabled), `read_graph` from a document, `compile_graph` to a control table, `graph_control` (one instance or a span), `AnimationSystem`'s graph surface, and the walker's stand/walk/run machine in `PlayerBody::tick_gait` replaced by `locomotion_graph` driven through it, its calls shown equal to the old machine's over 20,000 random ticks ([animation](subsystems/animation.md#graphs-s35-stage-1)). 2026-11-18, b69-S35-2: **stage 2 built** — the `Blend1D` with sync groups (a `phase_param`, or led by the clip of highest weight), the pose program (`GraphOp` 16 bytes, three registers, set/add/normalize so a blend at a threshold is its clip to the bit) evaluated op-major over batches by `PoseEvaluator`, every blend and cross-fade the normalized weighted sum, and the scheduled `animation.graph_control` and `animation.graph_sample` for graph-driven entities with no player; one worker against eight and any batch the same bytes, a blend pin taken on MSVC; the walker unchanged ([animation](subsystems/animation.md#graphs-the-pose-program-s35-stage-2)). 2026-11-18, b69-S35-3: **stage 3 built** — a graph's second machine as a layer over its first by an `Override` or `Additive` node (`GraphNode` v2, additive: `mask`, `reference`) with a weight times a float parameter, through a `GraphMask` (new record) by role or joint name with Unreal's Blend Depth at its edge, compiled per skeleton into full and partial joint runs (`GraphLayer`, 24 bytes); the program's two layer ops after the layer machine's own ops into a third or fourth register; override as ozz's per-joint weighted blend with the base at the complement, additive as ozz's difference and additive pass (rotation on the right, scale multiplied); a layer at weight 0 not evaluated; nothing added per instance; two pins taken on MSVC; the walker's crouch and head-look built as two test graphs, and their move written as a hunk for a later row ([animation](subsystems/animation.md#graphs-layers-and-masks-s35-stage-3), [the use](experiments/animation-graphs.md#the-use-it-is-for-the-walkers-crouch-and-head-look)). 2026-11-19, b70-S35-4: **two mends from the review of batch 69** — a graph replaced in place restarts its instances at the entries (`CompiledGraph::revision`, a hash of the compiled tables, carried by `AnimationGraphState` v2, additive, 72 bytes), and a `Clip` state's clip counted against the 65,535 pose clips, with the compile made n log n in its records; and the portable `sin_cos`/`atan2` moved from engine-view to `core/math` (`portable_trig.h`, a table pinned on MSVC) ([animation](subsystems/animation.md#graphs-s35-stage-1), [math](subsystems/math.md)); and **stage 4 but for the walker** — the inertialization curve after its sources (Bollo's GDC 2018 slides could not be read through the fetch tool; the rules are pinned to a reading of Unreal's implementation and to a derivation on the page), `InertialJoint` (48 bytes), capture and apply over poses portable to the bit (a pin taken on MSVC), the slot arena, and `Inertialize` through the compile, the control pass (a cross-fade with its duration negated: the target alone) and the scheduled sample pass, LOD1's cut opening no slot ([inertialization](subsystems/animation.md#graphs-inertialization-s35-stage-4), [the curve](experiments/animation-graphs.md#stage-4s-curve-read-and-derived)). 2026-11-19, b70-S35-4b: **stage 4's walker behind `animation.graph.inertialize_walker`, off** — on, the body is made graph-driven (`AnimationSystem::drive_by_graph`) by `locomotion_graph` with `Inertialize` transitions (`LocomotionClips::blend`) and its changes inertialized; off, it stays player-driven and no hash moves, because the program's cross-fade is not the player's bits (normalized sum against slerp, both fade clips put at the gait's phase by the host, f32 against f64 phase times duration: two values on the page); a phase-locked target one tick back sampled at the instance's last phases (`Inertial` 24 bytes); a no-driver case of the walker's three changes, continuous in value and in velocity to the quintic's bound and the target's bits after 0.2 s ([inertialization](subsystems/animation.md#graphs-inertialization-s35-stage-4)); stage 4's bench rows taken (upper bounds, a loaded machine: the cross-fade 23.8 ms and the inertialization 24.6 ms at 10⁴ instances, a quarter in flight: no saving at 23 joints, the apply costing what the second sample does); Bollo's GDC 2018 talk still not read (no transcript the fetch tool can reach). Not done: the graph-sampled walker as the default (moves every walker hash: the owner's call, after the walk with the tunable on) — stages 5 and 6 — the LOD programs and the shared pose, hot reload — a third machine (a second layer: `AnimationGraphState` v3), mesh-space additives and negative weights, the walker's walk-to-run as a `Blend1D` (the owner's to see) and the walker's crouch and look moved into the pose (needs the third machine, the walker graph-sampled, and IK for planted feet)) | S33 | M | design | [animation](subsystems/animation.md#graphs-s35-stage-1) |

A lake, a river or a sea has no tension boundary: its edge is the wave-displaced waterline against the ground's height, so the line advances and retreats with every wave, and the swash band it leaves is wet ground that dries with the time since it was last covered (W72's wet darkening under an age, or a decay by height above the mean line scaled by the wave's amplitude); the surface fades to the bottom over the last wavelength, not over `shore_m`; only a still small body (a puddle, a trough) keeps a meniscus. | S121 | The ears' wind counts the walker's own pace, and on a run it is the whole mix. Batch 73's staging walk flown by kind (`audio.world.solo`; a 3 m/s wind, a track at 4.5 m/s, the null backend's WAV read on the 105 dB scale): the ears alone 61.6 dBA, the crowns 48.2 and the beds 44.5 (49.7 together), the mix 61.9. So S114's mend holds on the forest's world (the beds 3.7 dB under the crowns) and still moved the mix only 1.3 dB (63.2 to 61.9), because on a moving listener the mix was never the trees. The ears' level goes as 40 log of the air's speed past the head, the wind less the head's own velocity: 46 dB while the walk stands at its start, 65 dB at its pace, steady, broadband and strongest at 250 to 500 Hz, which reads as the owner's "more like a waterfall than wind". And it is not one rule: in still air the same run is silent. To decide by the owner's ears, then build: whether a pace on foot counts at all (a listener does not attend to the wind of their own walking; a vehicle or a fall is another matter), the gain (`audio.world.ears_db`, −30 dB now), and one rule for still and moving air; a case that holds a runner in a light wind under the crowns round them. **The owner on batch 74's binaries** (2026-11-24; engine needs, item 178): "when running (holding shift and moving forward) it always sounds like a waterfall". So a pace on foot does count, and the law's sound is the wrong one for it: by the law a sprint at 5 m/s is 17 to 22 dB over standing, as two steady hard-panned noises, where wind at a running head is a low buffeting rumble that comes and goes with the head's own turbulence and the stride, most of it under a few hundred hertz. Build it from the literature on wind noise at the ear (its spectrum against speed and angle, what the two ears share), not from the gain. **Built in batch 75 and held from its merge (2026-11-25):** the voice is rebuilt on a measured spectrum with the two ears sharing the gust and each its own buffeting, read independently and mended, on the branch `b75-S121`; its spectrum's five coefficients were read from a public repository's source that proved to be GPL-3.0 (numbers only, no code), so it waited for the owner's word on merging it as it is or grounding the spectrum again from the paper first (T229). **The owner, 2026-11-25:** either, so long as the engine's Apache licence is untouched. Five numbers are data and not code, but only one of the two routes leaves no question to answer, so the spectrum is to be grounded again before the merge, first in batch 76: the coefficients fit here to the spectrum the paper publishes, the fit and its error on the grounding page, and the repository's five in neither the tree nor the history that reaches `main` (the row lands as commits that never held them). It still waits for the owner's ears on its gain (S130). **Grounded again and built in batch 76 (2026-11-25)** ([the grounding](experiments/world-sound.md#read-again-by-s121-the-wind-at-the-ears-as-it-is-heard), [world_sound](subsystems/world_sound.md#the-wind-at-the-ears-s121)): the work re-applied as new commits, every number from a published text or computed here. The spectrum is Nelke and Vary's (IWAENC 2014) by the five fixed coefficients Nelke's 2016 dissertation prints (Table 3.3: a fifth-order all-pole filter at 16 kHz), its power spectrum integrated by octave here — the most at 125 to 500 Hz, about 8 dB an octave above, brighter than the first build's; the conference paper shows them only as curves, so no fit was needed for them, and the voice was fitted to them: its octave table reads the exact octaves within 0.7 dB from 1.5 to 12 m/s past the head (1.5 at 7.1 m/s on the extended 8 kHz slope), and its low-pass's corner, fitted by least squares in dB to keep the voice on the shape from 62.5 Hz to 1 kHz, moved 300 → 500 Hz at 4.5 m/s (0.9 dB RMS over 3 to 12 m/s, 3.2 at most; at 300 Hz the refit voice read 6 dB under the shape at 1 kHz and a decibel under the law). The buffeting's frame is Nelke's 20 ms (Eq. 3.54), held through the frame; the two ears' coherence is Nelke's measured zero; the coherence decay rates, the smoothing window and the gains' magnitudes the first build had from the code are gone. Three pins moved with the spectrum (a run 0.66 of its power under 300 Hz where 0.87, its 4 kHz octave 38 dB under its loudest where over 40, the avenue's leaves 3 dB over the ears at 4 kHz where over 10; 18 at 8 kHz); at −16.5 dB a run was about 10 dB louder A-weighted than S112's. **Mended on 2026-11-25 and 26**: the buffeting is the dissertation's own published form, a Weibull draw (κ 2.71, Fig. 3.22) a 20 ms frame (Eq. 3.54) through the tree's deterministic logarithm and exponential, so no number and no method of the voice comes from the generator's code; the default gain is −26 dB, which keeps a run's A-weighted level at S112's, so at the default only the spectrum changed (under the sources' −19 to 0 dB: a mix choice); Korhonen's ear-side shape (flat to 300 Hz, 26 dB an octave above) set beside Table 3.3's on the grounding page, which is right for an ear not settled, the ear-side shape not built; a pinned session with the low-pass on, reproduced by GCC and Clang; a case at 60 ticks a second; the spread held against the buffeting off. Owed: the engine-view flights on this build (the card), and the owner's ears on the spectrum and the gain (S130) | in progress | S114 | S | design | [world_sound](subsystems/world_sound.md) |
| S122 | The world sound's refresh off the frame's thread, and the surf's records joined into lines (S113's hand-back, batch 74). With running water and a sea in earshot a refresh frame's `world.sound_ms` is 1.46 ms at the median and 2.22 ms at the 99th percentile on the forest staging, against the design's 1 ms: the surf there is 316 records, of which the cull before the path drops 8, and each one kept pays the path's terms. The refresh runs on a worker from a snapshot of the listener and the field, and the frame takes its result when it is ready (the mix is the audio thread's already); neighbouring surf records along one shore are joined into line sources before the path; and a case holds the frame's figure | done (2026-11-24, batch 75: the refresh in three parts in `systems/world_sound` — `gather` walks the host's tables into a snapshot on the tick's thread, `compute` costs it on the host's Efficiency pool (`SoundStage::set_jobs`; engine-view hands each loop's `page_jobs`), `apply` lands it at the gather's tick plus half a refresh period, the same bytes on a worker or not; a steady world starts no refresh, a late one is counted and claimed or waited for, every job a claimer; the surf's records joined into lines before the path (`join_surf`, 64 m squares by shore direction, breaker class, period and phase eighth, Σ W′L kept); the cases in `world_refresh_tests.cpp` and `engine_view.frame_loop`'s heard flight; [world_sound](subsystems/world_sound.md#the-refresh-off-the-frames-thread-s122). On the staging's grounded walk, main against this row: refresh frames 0.97 → 0.34 ms at the median, 2.7 → 0.68 at p99, 4.2 → 1.4 at most; the worker 0.77 ms a refresh; 2 of 1,079 late; the surf 3,920 records → 1,976 lines, 316 in reach → 153; the water heard +0.07 dB. Left on the frame's thread: the host's tables' walk, about 0.16 ms a gather. After the review's mend: the allocation case walks (343 refreshes through gather, schedule and apply, nothing allocated; the code needed no change), the late case is late and then the worker's by construction, a compute the job system ran on the tick's thread is counted late, a reopened stage starts clean, a stop settles the refresh without waiting on stale jobs, and the line's law is held in the stage. Not built: a cap on the snapshot's stretches and surf at the gather (bounded today by the hosts' 800 m reach: 45 KB on the staging), and the join's time in a release build (1.2 ms on 4,000 records in a debug one)) | S113 | M | design | [world_sound](subsystems/world_sound.md#the-refresh-off-the-frames-thread-s122) |

One reading: the host takes the session's world clock and sky clock each tick, the scene's epoch added, the water reads `FrameDesc::world_time_s` as every other moving medium does, and the second field goes | open | S118, S113 | S | routine | [world_sound](subsystems/world_sound.md) |

On the forest's world at a stand the beds still read 3.3 dB over the near crowns, and by octave from 125 Hz to 8 kHz the beds are 39.2, 41.8, 51.5, 52.3, 50.5, 49.3 and 47.2 dB, the crowns 30.4, 33.2, 48.2, 50.7, 46.8, 43.1 and 39.5, the ears 36.8, 34.9, 47.5, 50.4, 45.6, 39.5 and 29.4. The excess is in the spectrum more than the level: about 9 dB at 125 and 250 Hz and 6 to 8 dB at 4 and 8 kHz, under 4 dB between, so what reads as a waterfall while standing is the beds and not the ears. | T232 | A measure of difference nearer the eye's, and a comparison in motion (the owner, 2026-11-25). A shortcut's evidence today is a count of pixels past 2 of 255 on one still, which says neither whether an eye would notice nor what happens in motion, where most of a shortcut's faults live (flicker, crawl, a pop at a hand-off). Two tools. (1) A perceptual difference between two captures, grounded in the published measures: the still-image difference evaluators, and the video difference predictors that model contrast sensitivity over time and over eccentricity, which on the surround is the periphery. Each source's licence is read before a number or a line is taken (T229), and the model is built here from its paper, since the repository takes no Python. It is reported as a map and one pooled number beside the 2-of-255 count and `render.compare`, never in place of a pinned check. (2) A flight of one path under two settings or two binaries, frame for frame on one world clock (`tools/flythrough.ps1`), writing the difference per frame and its worst stretch, and a side-by-side reel only for a defect that has a name. Neither is a judge: a metric's or a model's reading of what an eye notices says where to look, the owner's eye decides, and a build is judged standing alone first. Stage 0 is the grounding page: which measures, their licences, what each claims to predict and at what viewing distance and field of view, and how each reads a shortcut already shipped (R213's cap at 32 fires first) | in progress (2026-11-25, batch 76, stage 0 done: the grounding, [perceptual-difference grounding](experiments/perceptual-difference-grounding.md) — the FLIP family, SSIM and why rendering moved past it, HDR-VDP-3, LPIPS and plan 12's rule on weights, the spatio-temporal CSFs (stelaCSF, castleCSF, elaTCSF, Krajancich et al.'s CFF over eccentricity), FovVideoVDP, ColorVideoVDP, tOF/tLP, CGVQM and VMAF, each with what it claims, its viewing geometry, its validation and its text's and code's licences apart: HDR-VDP's code GPL-2.0 and FovVideoVDP's CC BY-NC 4.0, so neither's code is a source; ColorVideoVDP's and castleCSF's MIT; the surround's geometry the engine would have to state; how each would read R213's cap, as reasoning; and a reading for the design kept apart, ColorVideoVDP from its paper first and an eccentricity-aware flicker map from elaTCSF beside it. `doc.promote` answering a digest of the proposal's records and the target's values at its conflicts and refusing 1004 "proposal moved" when a promotion's dry-run digest moved (`ReviewSubject` 2, `ReviewInfo` 3, `LedgerFile` 4 by a registered step, `ReviewListParams` 3, `ReviewsResult` 4, `ReviewCloseParams` 3, `PromoteParams` and `PromoteResult` 2, `SubjectState` 1); the queue design's attacks and the promotion's as cases in `ledger_tests.cpp` and `policy_tests.cpp`, ledgers at versions 2 and 3 read with their items unstamped; mended 2026-11-20 ("Bound the subject digest and read its files with the host let go", "Leave the layer stack out of the subject digest", "Tell a file outside the host's roots apart, and never call it moved", "Read the buffers and images a glTF subject names with it", "Hold a subject's listed files to the stored-path form" and the smaller ones beside them): four bounds as tunables, a raise past one refused, only regular files opened, the files read with the dispatch lock let go and the document compared after, the refusals that read no file first, the stack's lines dropped, a file outside the roots told apart and its close refused rather than called moved, a glTF's buffers and images read, the detail's filters refused, `decided_at` only for a digest sent, `subject.assets` held to the stored-path form (T174); mended again 2026-11-21 ("Admit a glTF's named files through the host's roots", "Bound what the subject digest collects, not only what it reads", "Say when a glTF's names cannot be followed, and refuse a raise", "Bound a glTF's names before reading them at a raise", "Digest a string no host could admit as one constant line", "Keep a digest's reads unless the subject itself moved", "Decide a close that compares nothing without the digest", "Answer an older raise's absolute asset by its index"): every file a glTF names admitted through the roots as a record's own is (T174) and answered by its index, never opened when refused; the files bound limiting what is collected and answered, a glTF's JSON bounded (`protocol.review.gltf_json_bytes`), a glTF whose names cannot be followed listed and a raise over it refused; a string no host could admit one constant line, told from a file some hosts cannot see by the stored spellings of the files the raise read (`ReviewInfo.raised_files`, which replaced the root names an earlier mend recorded: two hosts can name their roots alike and place them apart); a pass thrown away only when the subject moved, the item's own revision compared; a close that compares nothing decided without reading; an old absolute entry answered by its index; mended a fourth time 2026-11-21 ("Record what a raise read as files, not its roots' names", "Leave a glTF name's climb to the roots, and follow it or refuse", "Follow the files a .glb names, as a .gltf's" and five smaller): a raise made, and a close accepted, only where every file the subject names was read here or is honestly not there — the item recording the spellings its raise read (`ReviewInfo.raised_files`), a glTF's `..` at the front of a name left to the roots and one after a named segment refused, a refused name not followed, a `.glb`'s JSON chunk followed, an unfollowable glTF and a file there and not readable making a detail not comparable (`SubjectState.not_comparable`) and refusing a raise, a raced link answered as missing when its open succeeds and as the system's own refusal when the open itself is refused (T209), the handle's path converted as the roots convert theirs, and a close that compared nothing stating no subject (T209 opened for the admission's probe). Not done: the cost on a thousand-record subject measured on a quiet machine; a field marked as a path in the schema, so the digest reads what a record says is a file rather than guessing (T206); and what the digest follows is a `.gltf` or `.glb` by its suffix and nothing else, so a file that names others in another way — a glTF under another suffix, which the renderer imports by content, a `.clusters` container's image paths, a heightfield header's `data` — is hashed as its own bytes only and a rewrite of what it names is not seen (A68)) | A23 | M | design | [engine_shell](subsystems/engine_shell.md#what-a-close-is-tied-to) |

Past a threshold (a count of finished entries, or half the byte bound), the oldest finished tasks and closed items move under the same lock into an archive file beside `ledger.json`; their ids stay reserved so a drawn id never repeats one an archived entry had; `task.list` and `review.list` read the archive when asked; the bounds count the live file only; nothing open is ever archived, and a person's closed item is archived like any other | open | A23 | S | routine | [protocol](subsystems/protocol.md#the-task-ledger-and-review-items) |
